For inlet \(i\):

$$ T_i,\quad P_i,\quad RH_i $$

give:

$$ \boxed{ p_{v,i} = \frac{RH_i}{100}p_{ws}(T_i) } $$

Then:

$$ \boxed{ \omega_i = 0.621945 \frac{p_{v,i}} {P_i-p_{v,i}} } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ T_{dp,i} = p_{ws}^{-1}(p_{v,i}) } $$
6.23 Injection Moisture Rate

Dry-air mass flow:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{da,i} = \frac{\rho_iA_iU_i} {1+\omega_i} } $$

Vapor mass flow:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{v,i} = \dot m_{da,i}\omega_i } $$

or:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{v,i} = \frac{\rho_iA_iU_i\omega_i} {1+\omega_i} } $$
6.24 Injection With Volumetric Flow

If:

$$ \dot V_i $$

is directly specified:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_i=\rho_i\dot V_i } $$

then:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{da,i} = \frac{\rho_i\dot V_i} {1+\omega_i} } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{v,i} = \frac{\rho_i\dot V_i\omega_i} {1+\omega_i} } $$
6.25 Injection With Pressure Difference

For pressure-driven injection:

$$ \boxed{ \Delta P_i=P_i-P_{room} } $$

For an orifice:

$$ \boxed{ \dot V_i = C_dA_i \sqrt{ \frac{2|\Delta P_i|} {\rho_i} } } $$

Then:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{v,i} = \frac{\rho_i\dot V_i\omega_i} {1+\omega_i} } $$
6.26 Leaked-Air Condensation Contribution

Outside air may have:

$$ T_o,\quad P_o,\quad RH_o $$

Then:

$$ \boxed{ \omega_o = 0.621945 \frac{ (RH_o/100)p_{ws}(T_o) }{ P_o-(RH_o/100)p_{ws}(T_o) } } $$

Leakage flow:

$$ \boxed{ \dot V_L = C_dA_L \sqrt{ \frac{2|\Delta P|} {\rho_o} } } $$

Vapor infiltration:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{v,L} = \frac{\rho_o\dot V_L\omega_o} {1+\omega_o} } $$

This vapor enters the room and may subsequently condense.

6.27 Local Moisture Balance Including Condensation

The moisture equation becomes:

$$ \boxed{ \begin{aligned} \frac{\partial(\rho_{da}\omega)}{\partial t} + \nabla\cdot(\rho_{da}\mathbf u\omega) ={}& \nabla\cdot (\rho_{da}D_{eff}\nabla\omega) \\ &+ S_{in} + S_{leak} + S_{evap} - S_{cond} \end{aligned} } $$
6.28 Evaporation Condition

Evaporation occurs when:

$$ \boxed{ \omega<\omega_s } $$

provided liquid water is available:

$$ \boxed{ M_l>0 } $$

Therefore:

$$ \boxed{ \omega<\omega_s \quad\land\quad M_l>0 \Rightarrow evaporation } $$
6.29 Evaporation Rate

A simple transient formulation:

$$ \boxed{ S_{evap} = \rho_{da}k_{evap} \max(0,\omega_s-\omega) } $$

subject to:

$$ \boxed{ S_{evap}V\Delta t\le M_l } $$

to prevent evaporation of more liquid than exists.

6.30 Surface Evaporation

For a wet surface:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m''_{evap} = h_m \max (0,\rho_{v,s}-\rho_{v,\infty}) } $$

where:

$$ \rho_{v,s} = \frac{p_{ws}(T_s)} {R_vT_s} $$

and:

$$ \rho_{v,\infty} = \frac{p_v}{R_vT} $$

Therefore:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m''_{evap} = h_m \max \left[ 0, \frac{p_{ws}(T_s)}{R_vT_s} - \frac{p_v}{R_vT} \right] } $$
6.31 Evaporation Mass Rate
$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{evap} = A_s\dot m''_{evap} } $$
6.32 Condensate Inventory

Let:

$$ \boxed{ M_l(t) } $$

be the liquid-water inventory.

Then:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{dM_l}{dt} = \dot M_{cond} - \dot M_{evap} - \dot M_{drain} } $$

Discrete form:

$$ \boxed{ M_l^{n+1} = M_l^n + (\dot M_{cond} -\dot M_{evap} -\dot M_{drain})\Delta t } $$

with:

$$ \boxed{ M_l^{n+1}\ge0 } $$
6.33 Drainage

If condensate is drained:

$$ \boxed{ \dot M_{drain}=f(M_l) } $$

A simple model:

$$ \boxed{ \dot M_{drain} = \begin{cases} 0,&M_l<M_{crit}\\ k_{drain}(M_l-M_{crit}),&M_l\ge M_{crit} \end{cases} } $$
6.34 Latent Heat of Condensation

Condensation releases latent heat:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{latent,cond} = \dot m_{cond}L_v } $$

Volumetric energy source:

$$ \boxed{ S_{latent,cond} = S_{cond}L_v } $$

Thus:

$$ \boxed{ S_{latent,cond}>0 } $$

in the temperature/energy equation.

6.35 Latent Heat of Evaporation

Evaporation consumes energy:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{latent,evap} = \dot m_{evap}L_v } $$

Energy sink:

$$ \boxed{ S_{latent,evap} = -S_{evap}L_v } $$
6.36 Net Latent-Heat Source
$$ \boxed{ S_{latent} = L_v(S_{cond}-S_{evap}) } $$

Therefore:

$$ \boxed{ \text{Net latent energy} = \text{condensation release} - \text{evaporation consumption} } $$
6.37 Sensible Heat Associated With Condensation

The condensate also carries sensible energy depending on its temperature.

For condensate:

$$ \boxed{ Q_{s,cond} = m_{cond}c_{p,l}(T_{cond}-T_{ref}) } $$

Rate:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{s,cond} = \dot m_{cond}c_{p,l}(T_{cond}-T_{ref}) } $$

For evaporation:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{s,evap} = \dot m_{evap}c_{p,l}(T_{surface}-T_{ref}) } $$

However, this should only be included if the energy formulation explicitly tracks the liquid phase.

6.38 Total Energy Associated With Condensation

A more complete representation is:

$$ \boxed{ E_{cond,total} = E_{cond,sensible} + E_{cond,latent} } $$

with:

$$ \boxed{ E_{cond,sensible} = m_{cond}c_{p,l}(T_{cond}-T_{ref}) } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ E_{cond,latent} = m_{cond}L_v } $$

Therefore:

$$ \boxed{ E_{cond,total} = m_{cond} \left[ c_{p,l}(T_{cond}-T_{ref}) + L_v \right] } $$
6.39 Moist-Air Enthalpy

For incoming air:

$$ \boxed{ h = 1.006T+ \omega(2501+1.86T) } $$

in \(kJ/kg_{da}\).

Therefore inlet total energy rate:

$$ \boxed{ \dot E_i = \dot m_{da,i}h_i } $$
6.40 Latent Component of Inlet Energy

Approximate latent component:

$$ \boxed{ h_{latent,i} = \omega_iL_v } $$

Therefore:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{latent,i} = \dot m_{da,i}\omega_iL_v } $$
6.41 Sensible Component of Inlet Energy
$$ \boxed{ h_{sensible,i} \approx c_{p,ma}T_i } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{sensible,i} = \dot m_i c_{p,ma}(T_i-T_{ref}) } $$
6.42 Total Inlet Energy
$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{total,i} = \dot m_{da,i}h_i } $$

or approximately:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{total,i} = \dot Q_{sensible,i} + \dot Q_{latent,i} } $$

Do not add the same latent contribution again in Module 4 if the energy equation already uses the full moist-air enthalpy.

6.43 Specific Heat of Moist Air

Approximate:

$$ \boxed{ c_{p,ma} = \frac{ c_{p,da}+\omega c_{p,v} } {1+\omega} } $$

Alternatively, on a dry-air basis:

$$ \boxed{ c_{p,h} = c_{p,da} + \omega c_{p,v} } $$

This is useful when calculating sensible heating/cooling of moist air.

6.44 Condensation Energy Coupling to Temperature

Module 4 receives:

$$ \boxed{ S_{latent} = L_v(S_{cond}-S_{evap}) } $$

so the energy equation becomes:

$$ \boxed{ \rho c_p \frac{DT}{Dt} = \nabla\cdot(k\nabla T) + S_Q + L_v(S_{cond}-S_{evap}) } $$

Thus:

$$ \boxed{ S_{cond}\uparrow \Rightarrow T\uparrow } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ S_{evap}\uparrow \Rightarrow T\downarrow } $$
6.45 Condensation Feedback Loop

The numerical model therefore contains:

$$ \boxed{ \omega \rightarrow p_v \rightarrow RH \rightarrow \omega_s \rightarrow S_{cond} \rightarrow S_{latent} \rightarrow T \rightarrow p_{ws} \rightarrow \omega_s } $$

This is the important nonlinear feedback.

6.46 Cold-Surface Feedback

For a cold wall:

$$ \boxed{ T_s\downarrow \Rightarrow p_{ws}(T_s)\downarrow } $$

therefore:

$$ \boxed{ p_v-p_{ws}(T_s)\uparrow } $$

therefore:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{cond}\uparrow } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{latent,cond} = \dot m_{cond}L_v \uparrow } $$
6.47 Effect of Air Velocity on Surface Condensation

Velocity affects:

$$ Re=\frac{\rho UL}{\mu} $$

then:

$$ Re\rightarrow Sh $$

then:

$$ \boxed{ h_m=\frac{ShD}{L} } $$

and therefore:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m''_{cond} = h_m(\rho_{v,\infty}-\rho_{v,s}) } $$

Thus:

$$ \boxed{ U\uparrow \rightarrow Re\uparrow \rightarrow Sh\uparrow \rightarrow h_m\uparrow \rightarrow \dot m_{cond}\uparrow } $$

when the vapor-pressure driving force is positive.

6.48 Condensation at Different Locations

For \(N\) surfaces/locations:

$$ \boxed{ \dot M_{cond,total} = \sum_{j=1}^{N} \dot M_{cond,j} } $$

For each location:

$$ \boxed{ \dot M_{cond,j} = A_jh_{m,j} \max (0,\rho_{v,j}-\rho_{v,s,j}) } $$

where each location may have different:

$$ \boxed{ T_j,\ P_j,\ RH_j,\ U_j,\ T_{s,j},\ A_j } $$
6.49 Time-Dependent Condensation

All variables should be evaluated at:

$$ \boxed{ (x,y,z,t) } $$

Thus:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{cond} = \dot m_{cond}(x,y,z,t) } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ S_{cond} = S_{cond}(x,y,z,t) } $$

The accumulated condensate is:

$$ \boxed{ M_{cond}(x,y,z,t) = \int_0^t S_{cond}(x,y,z,\tau)\,d\tau } $$
6.50 Condensation Rate in a Numerical Cell

For cell \(i,j,k\):

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{cond,i,j,k}^{n} = S_{cond,i,j,k}^{n} V_{i,j,k} } $$

Then:

$$ \boxed{ M_{cond,i,j,k}^{n+1} = M_{cond,i,j,k}^{n} + \dot m_{cond,i,j,k}^{n}\Delta t } $$
6.51 Moisture Equation With Condensation and Evaporation

The final local equation is:

$$ \boxed{ \begin{aligned} \frac{\partial(\rho_{da}\omega)}{\partial t} + \nabla\cdot(\rho_{da}\mathbf u\omega) ={}& \nabla\cdot (\rho_{da}D_{eff}\nabla\omega) \\ &+ S_{in} + S_{leak} \\ &+ S_{evap} - S_{cond} \end{aligned} } $$

with:

$$ \boxed{ S_{cond} = \rho_{da}k_{cond} \max(0,\omega-\omega_s) } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ S_{evap} = \rho_{da}k_{evap} \max(0,\omega_s-\omega) } $$

subject to liquid-water availability.

6.52 Energy Equation Coupled to Module 6

The corresponding heat equation is:

$$ \boxed{ \rho c_p \left( \frac{\partial T}{\partial t} + \mathbf u\cdot\nabla T \right) = \nabla\cdot(k\nabla T) + S_Q + L_vS_{cond} - L_vS_{evap} } $$

This is the main connection between Module 6 and Module 4.

6.53 Global Water-Mass Balance

For the entire cold room:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{dM_v}{dt} = \dot M_{v,in} + \dot M_{v,leak} + \dot M_{evap} - \dot M_{cond} - \dot M_{v,out} } $$

where:

$$ \boxed{ M_v = \int_\Omega\rho_{da}\omega\,dV } $$
6.54 Global Liquid-Water Balance
$$ \boxed{ \frac{dM_l}{dt} = \dot M_{cond} - \dot M_{evap} - \dot M_{drain} } $$

Therefore the total water balance is:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{d}{dt}(M_v+M_l) = \dot M_{v,in} + \dot M_{v,leak} - \dot M_{v,out} - \dot M_{drain} } $$

This is an excellent conservation check for your simulation.

6.55 Condensation Conservation Error

Define:

$$ \boxed{ E_M = M_v(t)+M_l(t) - \left[ M_v(0)+M_l(0) + M_{in} - M_{out} - M_{drain} \right] } $$

A properly conservative numerical solver should give:

$$ \boxed{ E_M\rightarrow0 } $$

with grid and timestep refinement.

6.56 Condensation Risk Index

For dashboard purposes, define:

$$ \boxed{ CRI= \frac{\omega-\omega_s}{\omega_s} } $$

Interpretation:

$$ CRI<0 $$

→ unsaturated.

$$ CRI=0 $$

→ saturation.

$$ CRI>0 $$

→ supersaturation/condensation potential.

Another useful surface metric:

$$ \boxed{ \Delta T_{dp}=T_s-T_{dp} } $$

Then:

$$ \boxed{ \Delta T_{dp}<0 \Rightarrow \text{surface condensation risk} } $$
6.57 Condensation Intensity

Define:

$$ \boxed{ CI(x,y,z,t) = S_{cond}(x,y,z,t) } $$

Units:

$$ kg\,m^{-3}s^{-1} $$

For surfaces:

$$ \boxed{ CI_s = \dot m''_{cond} } $$

Units:

$$ kg\,m^{-2}s^{-1} $$
6.58 Condensation Energy Field

The latent heat release field:

$$ \boxed{ Q_{latent}(x,y,z,t) = L_vS_{cond}(x,y,z,t) } $$

Units:

$$ W/m^3 $$

Similarly, evaporation energy consumption:

$$ \boxed{ Q_{evap}(x,y,z,t) = -L_vS_{evap}(x,y,z,t) } $$
6.59 Total Phase-Change Energy
$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{phase} = \int_\Omega L_v(S_{cond}-S_{evap})\,dV } $$

Discrete:

$$ \boxed{ \dot Q_{phase} = \sum_{i,j,k} L_v (S_{cond}-S_{evap}) V_{i,j,k} } $$
6.60 Freezing / Frost Extension

If:

$$ T_s<0^\circ C $$

condensed water may freeze.

Let:

$$ M_{ice} $$

be ice mass.

Freezing:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{dM_{ice}}{dt} = \dot M_{freeze} } $$

Condensate balance becomes:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{dM_l}{dt} = \dot M_{cond} - \dot M_{evap} - \dot M_{freeze} - \dot M_{drain} } $$

Ice accumulation:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{dM_{ice}}{dt} = \dot M_{freeze} - \dot M_{sublimation} } $$

For the first version, you can keep frost formation as an extension rather than making it part of the initial solver.

6.61 Complete Module 6 Input Vector

For every injection/leakage location:

$$ \boxed{ \mathbf I_i= [ x_i,y_i,z_i, T_i,P_i,RH_i,\omega_i, U_i,\mathbf d_i, A_i,\dot V_i,\dot m_i, c_{p,i},h_i,\dot Q_i ] } $$

For each surface:

$$ \boxed{ \mathbf S_j= [ x_j,y_j,z_j, A_j,T_{s,j}, U_j,L_j, k_j,h_j,h_{m,j} ] } $$

For each timestep:

$$ \boxed{ t_n=n\Delta t } $$
6.62 Complete Module 6 Output Vector

At every:

$$ (x,y,z,t) $$

the module should provide:

$$ \boxed{ \begin{aligned} \mathcal C= [ &T,\ P,\ \mathbf u,\ \omega,\ RH,\ p_v,\ p_{ws},\\ &\omega_s,\ T_{dp},\\ &S_{cond},\ S_{evap},\\ &\dot m_{cond},\ \dot m_{evap},\\ &M_{cond},\ M_l,\ M_{ice},\\ &Q_{latent,cond},\ Q_{latent,evap},\\ &Q_{sensible},\ Q_{total},\\ &CRI,\ T_s-T_{dp} ] \end{aligned} } $$
6.63 Final Coupled Numerical Architecture

The complete relationship among Modules 3–6 becomes:

$$ \boxed{ T,P,\omega \rightarrow p_v,p_{ws},RH,\omega_s,T_{dp} } $$

then:

$$ \boxed{ T,\omega,\mathbf u \rightarrow \text{Moisture Transport} } $$

then:

$$ \boxed{ \omega-\omega_s \rightarrow S_{cond}/S_{evap} } $$

then:

$$ \boxed{ S_{cond}/S_{evap} \rightarrow L_v(S_{cond}-S_{evap}) } $$

then:

$$ \boxed{ L_v(S_{cond}-S_{evap}) \rightarrow T } $$

and simultaneously:

$$ \boxed{ T,P,\omega \rightarrow \rho \rightarrow \mathbf u } $$

So the complete transient feedback is:

$$ \boxed{ \mathbf u(x,y,z,t) \rightarrow \omega(x,y,z,t) \rightarrow RH(x,y,z,t) \rightarrow condensation/evaporation \rightarrow latent\ heat \rightarrow T(x,y,z,t) \rightarrow p_{ws} \rightarrow condensation/evaporation } $$
Master equations for Module 6

For your actual implementation, these are the core equations I would retain:

1. Moisture transport
$$ \boxed{ \frac{\partial(\rho_{da}\omega)}{\partial t} + \nabla\cdot(\rho_{da}\mathbf u\omega) = \nabla\cdot(\rho_{da}D_{eff}\nabla\omega) + S_{in}+S_{leak}+S_{evap}-S_{cond} } $$
2. Saturation condition
$$ \boxed{ \omega_s= 0.621945 \frac{p_{ws}(T)} {P-p_{ws}(T)} } $$
3. Condensation
$$ \boxed{ S_{cond} = \rho_{da}k_{cond} \max(0,\omega-\omega_s) } $$
4. Evaporation
$$ \boxed{ S_{evap} = \rho_{da}k_{evap} \max(0,\omega_s-\omega) } $$
5. Surface condensation
$$ \boxed{ \dot m''_{cond} = h_m \max(0,\rho_{v,\infty}-\rho_{v,s}) } $$
6. Surface evaporation
$$ \boxed{ \dot m''_{evap} = h_m \max(0,\rho_{v,s}-\rho_{v,\infty}) } $$
7. Latent heat
$$ \boxed{ S_{latent} = L_v(S_{cond}-S_{evap}) } $$
8. Condensate inventory
$$ \boxed{ \frac{dM_l}{dt} = \dot M_{cond} - \dot M_{evap} - \dot M_{drain} } $$
9. Inlet vapor mass
$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{v,in} = \frac{\rho A U\omega_{in}} {1+\omega_{in}} } $$
10. Leakage vapor mass
$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{v,leak} = \frac{\rho_o\dot V_{leak}\omega_o} {1+\omega_o} } $$
11. Leakage flow
$$ \boxed{ \dot V_{leak} = C_dA_{leak} \sqrt{\frac{2|\Delta P|}{\rho_o}} } $$
12. Energy coupling
$$ \boxed{ \rho c_p\frac{DT}{Dt} = \nabla\cdot(k\nabla T) + S_Q + L_vS_{cond} - L_vS_{evap} } $$
13. Surface mass-transfer coefficient
$$ \boxed{ h_m=\frac{ShD_{eff}}{L} } $$
14. Sherwood relation
$$ \boxed{ Sh=f(Re,Sc) } $$

with:

$$ \boxed{ Re=\frac{\rho UL}{\mu} } $$ $$ \boxed{ Sc=\frac{\mu}{\rho D_{eff}} } $$
One important modelling distinction

For your project, air-volume condensation and surface condensation should be separate submodels:

Module 6: Condensation
│
├── 6.1 Psychrometric saturation
│      ├── pws(T)
│      ├── ωs(T,P)
│      ├── RH
│      └── Tdp
│
├── 6.2 Bulk-air condensation
│      └── Scond(x,y,z,t)
│
├── 6.3 Surface condensation
│      ├── wall temperature
│      ├── dew-point criterion
│      ├── mass-transfer coefficient
│      └── m''cond
│
├── 6.4 Evaporation
│      ├── bulk evaporation
│      └── surface evaporation
│
├── 6.5 Condensate inventory
│      ├── liquid water
│      ├── drainage
│      └── optional ice/frost
│
├── 6.6 Latent heat
│      ├── condensation → +Lv
│      └── evaporation → −Lv
│
└── 6.7 Coupling
       ├── Airflow
       ├── Moisture
       ├── Temperature
       ├── Pressure
       └── Psychrometrics

This structure is substantially better than treating condensation as a simple if RH > 100: moisture = saturation operation. It gives you a genuine transient 3-D thermo-hygrometric model in which an inlet at \((x_1,y_1,z_1)\) with one \(T,RH,U\), a leakage opening at \((x_2,y_2,z_2)\) with another \(T,RH,\Delta P\), and cold surfaces at different temperatures can all produce different condensation rates:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_{cond} = \dot m_{cond}(x,y,z,t) } $$

which is exactly the field your dashboard ultimately needs to visualize.






















Module 7 — Airflow, Continuity, Momentum & Pressure Field

For the cold-storage numerical digital twin, Module 7 should calculate the transient 3-D air velocity and pressure fields:

$$ \boxed{ \mathbf u(x,y,z,t)=[u(x,y,z,t),v(x,y,z,t),w(x,y,z,t)] } $$

and

$$ \boxed{ P=P(x,y,z,t) } $$

These fields then drive moisture and heat transport from Modules 4–6.

The complete coupling is:

$$ \boxed{ P,T,\omega \rightarrow \rho,\mu \rightarrow \mathbf u \rightarrow T,\omega \rightarrow \text{condensation/evaporation} \rightarrow T } $$

The important point is that velocity, temperature, humidity, pressure, injection, leakage, condensation, evaporation and heat are coupled, but they should not all be treated as independent PDEs.

7.1 Primary Airflow Variables

At every cell:

$$ \boxed{ \mathbf Q(x,y,z,t) = [T,P,u,v,w,\omega] } $$

where:

\(T\) = temperature
\(P\) = absolute pressure
\(u\) = x-direction velocity
\(v\) = y-direction velocity
\(w\) = z-direction velocity
\(\omega\) = humidity ratio.

Derived velocity magnitude:

$$ \boxed{ U=\sqrt{u^2+v^2+w^2} } $$
7.2 Air Density

Air density must depend on the local thermodynamic state.

Using moist-air properties:

$$ \boxed{ \rho=\rho(T,P,\omega) } $$

A useful ideal-gas approximation is:

$$ \boxed{ \rho_{ma} = \frac{P} {R_{da}T_K(1+1.6078\omega)} (1+\omega) } $$

where:

$$ T_K=T+273.15 $$

and:

$$ R_{da}\approx287.05\;J/(kg\,K) $$
7.3 Dry-Air Density
$$ \boxed{ \rho_{da} = \frac{P} {R_{da}T_K(1+1.6078\omega)} } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ \rho_{ma}=\rho_{da}(1+\omega) } $$

This gives the coupling:

$$ \boxed{ T,P,\omega\rightarrow\rho } $$
7.4 Dynamic Viscosity

Air viscosity:

$$ \boxed{ \mu=\mu(T,\omega) } $$

For a simplified model:

$$ \boxed{ \mu\approx\mu(T) } $$

Kinematic viscosity:

$$ \boxed{ \nu=\frac{\mu}{\rho} } $$
7.5 Continuity Equation

The fundamental mass-conservation equation is:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{\partial\rho}{\partial t} + \nabla\cdot(\rho\mathbf u) = S_m } $$

where \(S_m\) is a volumetric mass source/sink.

For no volumetric mass generation:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{\partial\rho}{\partial t} + \nabla\cdot(\rho\mathbf u) =0 } $$
7.6 Expanded 3-D Continuity Equation
$$ \boxed{ \frac{\partial\rho}{\partial t} + \frac{\partial(\rho u)}{\partial x} + \frac{\partial(\rho v)}{\partial y} + \frac{\partial(\rho w)}{\partial z} = S_m } $$

This equation ensures that the injected and leaked air are properly accounted for.

7.7 Constant-Density Continuity

If the room is treated as incompressible:

$$ \boxed{ \nabla\cdot\mathbf u=0 } $$

or:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{\partial u}{\partial x} + \frac{\partial v}{\partial y} + \frac{\partial w}{\partial z} =0 } $$

This is often appropriate for a low-Mach-number cold-storage airflow model.

7.8 Why Density Should Still Be Calculated

Even if the velocity solver uses:

$$ \nabla\cdot\mathbf u=0 $$

you should still calculate:

$$ \boxed{ \rho=\rho(T,P,\omega) } $$

because density is required for:

$$ \dot m=\rho\dot V $$ $$ Re=\frac{\rho UL}{\mu} $$ $$ \Delta P\sim\frac12\rho U^2 $$

and heat/moisture transport.

7.9 Continuity With Injection

For an injection source \(i\):

$$ \boxed{ S_{m,i} = \frac{\dot m_i}{V_{source,i}} } $$

For multiple injection points:

$$ \boxed{ S_m = \sum_{i=1}^{N_{in}} S_{m,i} } $$
7.10 Injection Mass Flow Rate

If velocity is specified:

$$ \boxed{ \dot V_i=A_iU_i } $$

and:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_i=\rho_iA_iU_i } $$
7.11 Injection Direction

Let:

$$ \hat{\mathbf d}_i = (d_{x,i},d_{y,i},d_{z,i}) $$

with:

$$ \boxed{ d_{x,i}^2+d_{y,i}^2+d_{z,i}^2=1 } $$

Then:

$$ \boxed{ \mathbf u_i = U_i\hat{\mathbf d}_i } $$

so:

$$ u_i=U_id_{x,i} $$ $$ v_i=U_id_{y,i} $$ $$ w_i=U_id_{z,i} $$
7.12 Injection Specified by Volumetric Flow

If:

$$ \dot V_i $$

is specified:

$$ \boxed{ U_i=\frac{\dot V_i}{A_i} } $$

Then:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_i=\rho_i\dot V_i } $$
7.13 Pressure-Driven Injection

For pressure-driven injection:

$$ \boxed{ \Delta P_i=P_i-P_{room} } $$

For an orifice:

$$ \boxed{ \dot V_i = C_dA_i \sqrt{ \frac{2|\Delta P_i|} {\rho_i} } } $$

Therefore:

$$ \boxed{ \dot m_i = \rho_iC_dA_i \sqrt{ \frac{2|\Delta P_i|} {\rho_i} } } $$
7.14 Momentum Equation

The general transient 3-D momentum equation is:

$$ \boxed{ \frac{\partial(\rho\mathbf u)}{\partial t} + \nabla\cdot(\rho\mathbf u\otimes\mathbf u) = -\nabla P + \nabla\cdot\boldsymbol\tau + \rho\mathbf g + \mathbf S_u } $$

This is the main equation for Module 7.

\(p = mv\)
